import sys

def is_palindrome(text: str) -> bool:
    # Normaliza el texto: convierte a minúsculas y elimina espacios
    normalized = text.lower().replace(" ", "")
    return normalized == normalized[::-1]

def chunk_sum(values: list[int], chunk_size: int) -> list[int]:
    result = []
    for i in range(0, len(values), chunk_size):
        chunk = values[i:i + chunk_size]
        result.append(sum(chunk))
    return result

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Ejemplo de uso
    print(is_palindrome("A man a plan a canal Panama"))
    print(chunk_sum([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], 2))
```

def is_palindrome(text: str) -> bool:
    # Convert to lowercase and remove non-alphanumeric characters for a simple palindrome check
    cleaned = ''.join(char.lower() for char in text if char.isalnum())
    return cleaned == cleaned[::-1]

def chunk_sum(values: list[int], chunk_size: int) -> list[int]:
    # Split the list into chunks of size chunk_size and sum each chunk
    result = []
    for i in range(0, len(values), chunk_size):
        chunk = values[i:i + chunk_size]
        result.append(sum(chunk))
    return result
```

    normalized = text.lower().replace(" ", "")
    return normalized == normalized[::-1]

    result = []
    for i in range(0, len(values), chunk_size):
        chunk = values[i:i + chunk_size]
        result.append(sum(chunk))
    return result

def is_palindrome(text: str) -> bool:
    # Convert to lowercase and remove non-alphanumeric characters for a simple palindrome check
    cleaned = ''.join(char.lower() for char in text if char.isalnum())
    return cleaned == cleaned[::-1]

def chunk_sum(values: list[int], chunk_size: int) -> list[int]:
    # Split the list into chunks of size chunk_size and sum each chunk
    result = []
    for i in range(0, len(values), chunk_size):
        chunk = values[i:i + chunk_size]
        result.append(sum(chunk))
    return result
```

import sys

def mean(values):
    if not values:
        raise ValueError("Cannot compute mean of an empty list")
    
    return sum(values) / len(values)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    # Simple test to verify functionality
    try:
        print(mean([1, 2, 3]))  # Should output 2.0
        print(mean([]))         # Should raise ValueError
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Exception: {e}")
```

```
bench-output/summary.py
import json

def top_scores(path: str, limit: int) -> list[int]:
    with open(path, 'r') as f:
        records = json.load(f)
    
    # Sort by score descending and get top scores
    sorted_records = sorted(records, key=lambda x: x['score'], reverse=True)
    return [record['score'] for record in sorted_records[:limit]]
```
